Let's think through this problem carefully.

Problem: Find a step function h: [0,2] → [0,1] that minimizes C₅ = max_k ∫ h(x)(1-h(x+k)) dx, with normalization ∫₀² h = 1. Discretized: h has n samples on [0,2], sum(h)*dx = 1, dx = 2/n. C₅ = max(np.correlate(h, 1-h, mode='full')) * dx.

Previous record: C₅ ≤ 0.38085. Previous program achieved 0.380907. Goal: improve to at most 0.38085 (or better).

Key observations:

1. Objective function: for each cyclic/linear shift k (in the indices of h? actually np.correlate is non-cyclic and full mode gives 2n-1 taps of lags k = -(n-1), ..., n-1)

corr[m] = Σᵢ h[i] (1-h[i + m - (n-1)])? Let's check the convention: np.correlate(a, v, 'full')[k] = Σᵢ a[i+k]·conj(v[i])? numpy docs: c_k = Σ_n a_{n+k} · conj(v_n). That is, corr[m] = Σᵢ h[i+m] (1-h[i]) with the valid overlap range. This being symmetric, as a multiset it's symmetric? Not exactly, but due to the integrand's symmetric structure, corr[m] = corr[-m]? Let's check: corr[m] = Σᵢ valid h[i+m](1-h[i]), the range of i is where both are valid. corr[-m] = Σᵢ h[i-m](1-h[i]) = change of variable j = i - m... Let's set m>0: corr[m] = Σ_{i=0}^{n-1-m} h[i+m] - h[i+m]h[i]. corr[-m] = Σ_{i=m}^{n-1} h[i-m](1-h[i]) = Σ_{j=0}^{n-1-m} h[j](1-h[j+m]) = Σ (h[j] - h[j]h[j+m]). Hmm, does this differ from corr[m] = Σ (h[i+m] - h[i]h[i+m])? Actually they are equal only if Σ h[i+m] = Σ h[i] (each partial sum), which doesn't hold generally. But wait, the integral form: ∫ h(x)(1-h(x+k)) dx and ∫ h(x)(1-h(x-k)) dx — using periodic extension? No, the window is. Actually the true continuous problem on [0,2]: erf? The problem says ∫₀? Hmm, the original problem: C_5 = max_k ∫ h(x)(1-h(x+k)) dx — the integration is over the common support. Due to the sum constraint and symmetry of overlap, the two are generally equal? For periodic functions on a circle they'd be the same. In the discrete correlate full use, the anti-constraint is a non-cyclic (linear) one? Actually np.correlate, full mode: no wrap. But the problem statement says exactly C₅ = max over k of np.correlate(h, 1-h, mode="full") * dx is computed. So the optimizer should treat exactly that functional.

2. Interesting structure: corr[m] = Σ h[i+m](1-h[i]) = Σ h[i+m] over the overlap range − Σ h[i+m] h[i]. Let S_k = partial sum of h (with offset). And A_k = Σ h[i]h[i+k] (auto-correlation of h). Then corr[k] = S_+ − A_k, where S_+ = Σ_{i=0}^{n-1-k} h[i+k] (with k≥0).

Note: Σ_{i=0}^{n-1-k} h[i+k] is not a constant (sum of last n-k entries... no wait, i=0..n-1-k, i+k is from k to n-1? i+k covers [k, n-1]. That's the sum of the suffix from k to n-1. Hmm, that depends on where the 1s sit — the ordering matters! Haha, interesting: contents of h matter via suffix sums and overlap of the overlap products.

Alternative reading: Integral ∫₀² h(x)(1-h(x+k)) dx is over x ∈ [0, 2] ∩ where both are defined; h is defined on [0,2], and h(x+k), if out of range... probably define h ≡ 0 outside [0,2]? In that case ∫ h(x)(1-h(x+k)) dx over all x = ∫_{[0,2]\backslash?} ... = Σ over the common window, but 1-h(x+k) = 1 when x+k is outside the support (zero difference). That's different from correlate full! Under out-of-range = 0: ∫ h(x)(1-h(x+k)) = Σ_{i: i ∈ W, i+k ∈ W}? No: for x + k outside [0,2], h(x+k) = 0, so the integrand = h(x)·1 = contribution from x. So total = Σ_i h[i] − Σ_{i,i+k both valid} h[i]h[i+k] = total_sum − A_k = n/2 − A_k. This is cleaner and the cyclic/linear shift invariance is restored: C = max_k (n/2 − A_k), A_k = Σ_i h[i] h[i+k] (only valid pairs) = cyclic autocorrelation only when both are in range...

Hmm. But the instruction is explicit: "the evaluation computes: C₅ = max(np.correlate(h, 1-h, mode="full") * dx)". That is the authoritative definition for this task. The previous program used exactly that. The expected harness presumably computes it the same way. Note: the difference between these two definitions: correlate-full version's corr[m] = Σ_{i=0}^{n-1-m} h[i+m](1-h[i]) = suffix_sum(m) − A_m, where suffix_sum(m) = Σ_{j=m}^{n-1} h[j]. n/2 - A_m? vs suffix_sum(m) − A_m. Difference: suffix_sum(m) − n/2. So if the tail has more mass, the correlate-full objective yields a higher value at positive lags. Interesting.

So actually, to minimize max_k (suffix_sum(k) - A_k), it might be beneficial to place h's mass near the beginning (small suffix sums) — for h as a set of 1s, the prefix is "better". Hmm, wait. A_m = overlap of 1s at m positions. Σ in the full correlate: corr[m] = Σ_{i=0}^{n-1-m} h[i+m](1-h[i]) = (sum of h from index m to n-1) − A_m. corr[-m] = (sum of h from 0 to n-1-m) − A_m.

We minimize the max over m≥0 of both forms = max_m (max of prefix_sum(n-m), suffix_sum(m)) − A_m.

Hmm, OK. That's the accurate objective. We should design the optimizer around that.

Insight: we can rewrite. Let F(k) = Σ_{i} h[i] h[i+k] (standard full autocorrelation with boundary effects). Let P(k) = Σ_{i=0}^{n-1-k} h[i] = volume of prefix of length n-k, S(k) = Σ_{i=k}^{n-1} h[i] = volume of suffix of length n-k. Then objective terms: T(k) = S(k) − F(k) and T(−k) = P(k) − F(k). Maximization of k over both signs.

Note P(k) + S(k) = n/2 + F?? No: P(k) = sum of length n-k prefix. S(k) = sum of length n-k suffix. P(k) − S(k) = sum of prefix − sum of suffix = (Σ_0^{n-1-k} h[i]) − (Σ_k^{n-1} h[i]) = (Σ_0^{k-1} h[i]) − (Σ_{n-k}^{n-1} h[i]). So if the first k bins and last k bins have equal mass, T(k) = T(−k) holds, giving (n/2 − ...). Interesting: if h is "positionally symmetric" in the boundary sense, the two equal.

Also note: F(k) ≤ and also consider cyclic autocorrelation R_c(k) = cyclic Σ_i h[i] h[i+k mod n]. Relation: R_c(k) − F(k) = Σ_{i: i+k ≥ n} h[i] h[i+k−n] = cyclic wrap term. Interesting.

Total overlap cost: Σ_k≥0 [T(k) + T(−k)] = Σ_k [P(k) + S(k) − 2F(k)]... let's compute: Σ_{k=1}^{n-1} T(k) = Σ_k S(k) − Σ_k F(k) = Σ_{k} (n/2 − P(n-k)??) Hmm. Σ_{k=1}^{n-1} S(k) = Σ_{k=1}^{n-1} Σ_{i=k}^{n-1} h[i] = Σ_{i=1}^{n-1} i h[i]. Similarly Σ P(k) = Σ (n−i) h[i]. Sum = for i≥1, (i + n − i) h[i] = n Σ_{i=1}^{n-1} h[i] ≈ n(n/2 − h[0]).

Capacity: minimizing the max is like minimizing a weighted average with emphasis on the peaks.

Another consideration: h values in [0,1] are continuous. Presumably the optimum has many fractions (not just 0/1). The previous approach uses SLSQP on up to 320 variables, smooth max approximation, sub-gradient, coordinate descent.

Ideas for improvement:
- Better resolution search: a small n is computationally advantageous. The target 0.38085 × dx is in the units of n/2 (schematically). attain minimum overlap? Each T(k) ≥ 0. C5 ~ 0.38 means max overlap ≈ 0.38·n/2 units? Let's see: corr[k]·(2/n) ≤ 0.38085 → corr[k] ≤ 0.190425 n. Since sum h = n/2, A(k) ≥ S(k) − 0.190425 n.

- Since the objective is piecewise-linear in h (max of linear forms!), the problem is a linear program! Check: T(k) = S(k) − F(k): S(k) is linear in h. F(k) = Σ_i h[i] h[i+k] is bilinear — not linear. Damn. Even if we binary-constrain, it's a quadratic.

But wait — the objective is max_k of bilinear forms. For fixed support selection... overall nonconvex.

However, for 0/1 (binary step, a "nice step" function), the problem is: select a subset A ⊂ {0..n-1}, |A| = n/2, minimizing max_k (S(k) − |A ∩ (A+k)|). Is minimizing a quadratic-overlinear-? max is computationally hard, but local search might be good enough.

Time budget is 1300s, 1 CPU. The previous program spends most of the time on SLSQP for n up to 320, maxiter 500, which is slow (~n³ operations per iteration, plus finite-difference n+1 evaluations at objective O(n)) — too slow. A better strategy: use a fast direct local search via O(n) incremental per-move evaluation.

Key computational trick: F(k), the autocorrelation lag array, can be recomputed in O(n²) per h change, but a bit flip changes all lags by O(n)? Actually, flipping h[i] from 0 to 1 changes F(k) by +h[i+k] (for all k) → O(n) update per flip. So we can maintain the lag array F at O(n) per flip. Then T(k), max = O(n). Greedy/local search: try pairs of moves? For monotone improvement, flipping a single 1 to 0 changes the sum constraint. Maintain sum = n/2, so the move is a swap: h[i]: 1→0, j: 0→1. The change in F(k) is: F'(k) = F(k) − h[i+k] − h[j+k] + h[i+k]h[j+k]... let's do it carefully: h' differs from h at i (1→0) and j (0→1). F'(k) = Σ_t h'[t] h'[t+k]. Against F(k), terms where t ∈ {i,i−k} or t+k ∈ {i,i−k}... simply recompute: contribution involving i or j. Simpler: for lag k, the changed indices are in {i,j} and {i-k, j-k, i+k, j+k}. Per-lag change = (h'[a]h'[a+k] − h[a]h[a+k]) summed over shift-colliding t: t ∈ {i, j, i−k, j−k} = {t : t or t+k ∈ {i,j}} counted once each. In practice, for each k: affected t: for each a ∈ {i,j}: t = a, t = a+k (if in range), t = a−k (if in range). Up to 6 terms. So per-swap delta F (all lags) O(n). S(k), P(k) also O(n) update? S(k) = Σ_{t=k}^{n-1} h[t]: subtract 1 at i (if i ≥ k), add 1 at j (if j ≥ k). So O(n) for the suffix mask but precomputation possible? Actually S(k) change per swap: ΔS(k) = −[i ≥ k] + [j ≥ k]. This is a step function of k: for k ≤ i, −1; for i < k ≤ j, that's... piecewise-constant in k — O(n) to apply but cheap. P(k) = Σ_{t=0}^{n-1-k} h[t]: ΔP(k) = [i ≤ n−1−k] − [j ≤ n−1−k] → piecewise in k. So per swap we can compute new T(k) in O(n), n elements, each O(1) operations. So full candidate evaluation per swap is O(n) ≈ 160–512 ops — super cheap. 10^7 swaps/sec? Realistic numpy: each swap candidate evaluation vectorized O(n): 512 ops via numpy; ~10–30μs → ~30–60k candidates/sec, ~40 million in 1300s. Binary (a deterministic annealing?) local search with state 2^?; plus continuous relaxation (fractional search) for bits near 0.5.

Comparison with previous: SLSQP at n=320, objective evaluate via np.correlate O(n²) = 100k flops, per SLSQP iter O(n) → 320 evaluations... actually SLSQP uses finite differences: ~n evaluations per iter, each O(n²) → 320·100k = 3.2e7 per iter — slow (~seconds per iter). So the previous runs maybe hundreds of iters in 1300s. Our local search can jump billions of evaluations → probably better.

So implement a fast local search:

State: h ∈ {0,1}^n, sum = n/2 (n even), maintain:
- F[k] for k=0..n−1? All k = −(n−1)..(n−1) in correlate full. Lag: diff = m − (n−1), m ∈ 0..2n−2. For k≥0: F(k) = Σ h[i+k]h[i], i=0..n−1−k. For k<0: F(k) = F(−k)? ? F(−k) = Σ h[i]h[i−k] (valid) = Σ h[i+k] h[i]... over valid range = same as F(k). Wait: F(−k): Σ_i h[i](1... no, corr symmetric part. Auto-correlation F(k) = Σ_i h[i+k] h[i] over valid i (all i, h outside is 0) is even in k. Yes, F(k) = F(−k). But T differs: T(+k) = S(k) − F(k), T(−k) = P(k) − F(k), S(k)=Σ_{i=k}^{n−1} h[i], P(k)=Σ_{i=0}^{n−1−k} h[i].

So maintain an array T of length 2n−1, index m = k + (n−1): T[m] = (S or P) − F. max_C5 = max T · dx.

Per swap (i:1→0, j:0→1):
- ΔF(k) over k≥0 (also applied to negative side): for k=0..n−1: change = (h[j+k]−h[i+k])·h[...] hmm, need to recompute affected products: F(k) = Σ_{t=0}^{n−1−k} h[t+k] h[t]. The changed indices are {i,j}. Product change at (t, t+k): h_inv = −h[i], h_jnv = +1(j): ΔF(k) = −h[i+k]h[i]? Carefully, new = old − (old contribution of pair involving i) + (new contribution of pair involving j).

Old pair terms containing i: (t=i) → h[i] h[i+k] (if i+k < n; but h[i]=1 → contributes h[i+k]); (t = i−k) → h[i-k+k] h[i-k] = 1·h[i-k] (if i−k ≥ 0). New terms at j: (t=j) → h' j+... h'[j] = 1 → h[j+k] (if j+k<n); (t=j−k) → h[j] h[j-k] = h[j-k] (if j−k ≥ 0). However if a pair is shared {i,j}? k = j−i → t: (i, j) covered in both: old h[i]h[j] = 1·0 = 0; new: h'[i]h'[j] = 0·1 = 0. Naive Δ = −(h[i+k]+h[i-k]) + (h[j+k]+h[j-k]); for k = j−i: old pairs: t=i: h[i]h[j] = 0; t=j−k=i: h[j]h[i] = 0 (double-counted — same pair counted twice!). i.e., pairs containing i twice (t=i and t=i+k=i... when k=j−i, t=j−k = i and (t,t+k)=(i,j)); once. Naive sum over i's two pair incidents counts pair (i,j) twice when it contains j... Wait t=i: pair (i, i+k=j): old h[i]h[j] = 1·0=0; incidence t=j−k=i: pair (i,j) again: old 0. Both same pair, old contribution 0, after both removed → still 0 terms → net 0; but my double subtraction subtracts h[j]+h[i] twice = 2. Similarly j side adds pairs (j, j+k) and (j−k, j); when k=j−i the first: pair (j,i)? (t=j, t+k=j+k) → not (i,j) for k=j−i: (j, 2j−i). The second: (j−k, j) = (i, j): new contribution h'[i]h'[j] = 0·1 = 0. Same pair double-added: my formula adds h[j... at k=j−i: j side: h[j+k] = h[2j−i], h[j−k]=h[i]=1 (old value!). Subtle. Cleanest: for all k, recompute F(k) for the 4 (≤6)-linking terms each old/new:

For given k ≥ 0, terms t ∈ {i, j, i−k, j−k} ∩ [0, n−1−k], with t+k < n guaranteed. For each such t (dedup), Δ += h_new[t+k]h_new[t] − h[t+k]h[t]. Dedup via set; 2–4 distinct t's, each O(1).

Simpler O(n) per swap vectorized in numpy: h_new = h.copy(); h_new[i]=0; h_new[j]=1; F_new = np.correlate? That's O(n²) — too slow for many candidates. But with ~1M candidates/sec + O(n) target runtime: pure numpy per candidate F-new via correlate impossible. The structured approach (up to 6 terms) is fine: ~30 flops Python-level per k, 160 k per lag → ~5–10 μs; 1M candidates/s → 5–10 seconds.

Implementation details — exact objective matcher:
Array of length n (0..2n−2), T: for m (m−(n−1) = k ≥ 0): T = S(k) − F(k), S(k) = Σ_{i=k}^{n−1} h_i; for k < 0 (m < n−1): T = P(|k|) − F(|k|), P(k) = Σ_{i=0}^{n−1−k} h_i. F(k) = Σ_{i: i,i+k ∈ [0,n)} h_i h_{i+k} (k≥0), even.
So construct: F = array(n); for k in 1..n−1: F[k] = Σ h[i]h[i+k]. Vectorize per k correlation... initial construction O(n²) once; per swap update: ΔF(k): as derived:
new = old − (h[i+k]∈[n] + h[i−k]∈[n]) + (h[j+k]∈[n] + h[j−k]∈[n]), special: pair counted twice when j−k == i? Wait: j's incident pair: t = j: pair (j, j+k); t = j−k: pair (j−k, j). If j−k == i, i's both incidents? no: i's incident is r = i, i−k. j is... Case "pair (i, j)": j = i+k (k = j−i): i's incident t = i: pair (i, i+k) = (i,j): old contribution h_i h_j = 1·0 = 0; my subtraction is h[i+k] = h[j] = 0 → OK (no double count, old 0 in formula... −a with a=0 → 0 ✓). j's incident t=j−k=i: pair (i, j): new contribution h'_i h'_j = 0·1 = 0; my formula adds d = h[j−k] = h[i] = 1 → +1 wrong. So correction: k = j−i (and i < j): ΔF(k) −= 1. Similarly case pair (i,j) with k = i−j?? k>0 only, k = |i−j| pairing: if j > i, k = j−i handled; if i > j, then pair (i,j) is i's incident? t = i−k: pair (i−k, i) = (j, i) (k = i−j): old contribution h_j·h_i = 0·1 = 0; subtraction is c = h[i−k] = h[j] = 0 → ✓. j's incident: t = j: pair (j, j+k) = (j, i): new contribution h'_j h'_i = 1·0 = 0; formula adds b = h[j+k] = h[i] = 1 → +1 wrong → also k = i−j (i>j) correction −1. So correction: if k == |i−j|: ΔF(k) −= 1. Nice: single rule.
Maintained S(k) & P(k): S(k) = suffix from k: S(k) = total − prefix(k): S(k) change: −1 if k ≤ i; +1 if k ≤ j → ΔS(k) = [k≤j] − [k≤i]. P(k) = prefix(n−k): ΔP(k) = [i ≤ n−1−k] − [j ≤ n−1−k] = [k ≤ n−1−i] − [k ≤ n−1−j]. All arrays over k ∈ 1..n−1.

So per candidate: np.array get: dS, dP via slicing ops: dS[1:j] = 1?? dS(k) = [k≤j] − [k≤i] (j>i): = 1 on [i+1..j], else 0. dP: k ≤ n−1−j: 0; n−1−j < k ≤ n−1−i: −1. So deltas are sparse intervals! dS: +1 on [i+1, j]; dP: −1 on [n−j, n−1−i] (integer k). ΔF is a long array (slicing h). So: newF = F + (b − a) + (d − c) − δ; new_plus = S + dS − newF... Wait T_plus(k) = S(k) − F(k): ΔT_plus = dS − ΔF; T_minus(k) = P(k) − F(k): ΔT_minus = dP − ΔF. Overall max over np.concatenate (timeout doubles: we maintain array L[1..n−1] positive side & M[1..n−1] negative side; C5max = max(max(L), max(M))·dx.

Implementation details — 2-opt search in numpy:

def build_T(n, h):
  # h is 0/1 float or int array
  F = np.zeros(n); for k in 1..n-1: F[k] = np.dot? Vectorize the loop: for k in range(1, n): F[k] = np.count_nonzero((h[:-k] & h[k:]) if binary int) — np.logical_and count ~10μs·(k loop 160 = 1.6ms/initial build). Accumulate bit trick for construction: 64-bit: F[k] = (A & (A>>k)).bit_count(): initial build fast. Tplus[k] = S(k) − F(k), S(k) = sum h[k:] = total − prefix(k). So Tplus/minus arrays.

Maintain: h (int8), A? Or numpy. Keep numpy h; per-swap ΔF vector:
k = 1..n−1 (int arrays held)
a_ = (k ≤ n−1−i)·h[i+k] → v = h[i+1:]; a = take a if k < n−i else 0
Implementation:
  AF = np.zeros(n, float); AF[1: n−i] = h[i+1: n]?? i+k, k ≤ n−1−i: k = 1..n−1−i → h[i+1: n]. So AF[1:n−i] = h[i+1:n] length n−i−1 ✓
  B similar [1:n−j] = h[j+1:n]
  CF[1:i+?] c(k) = h[i−k], k = 1..i: k ascending → h[i−1], h[i−2], ..., h[0] = h[:i][::-1]: CF[1:i+1] = h[:i][::-1]
  DF[1:j+1] = h[:j][::-1]
  deltaF = B − CF + DF: np.subtract(B, AF, out=new): 3-way op → AF_copy + (B−C) + D → 2 operand → ΔF = (B − C) + (D − A)? np: t1 = B - A; t2 = D - C; d = t1 + t2 (n array) → 3 ops ~(n length 160 ~ 4μs ✓). If abs(i−j) < n: d[abs(i−j)] -= 1.
  dS: intervals: [i+1, j] += 1 → cumulative
  dP: [n−1−j, n−1−i] inclusive + (−1)?? → n−1−j < k ≤ n−1−i → index n−1−j + 1 = n−j to n−1−i: length? (n−1−i) − (n−j) + 1 = j − i ✓: dP = np.zeros; dP[n−j: n−i] −= 1 (i<j: n−j > n−i, hmm: n−j < n−i (j > i) ✓ slice [n−j : n−i], endpoint-exclusive n−i is n−1−i ✓). j > i assumed (swap exchanges 1 at i, 0 at j).
  newTplus = Tplus + (dS − d)
  maxP = newTplus.max(); newTminus = Tminus + (dP − d); maxM = newTminus.max(); cand = max(maxP, maxM).
  If cand < bestT (tol): update F? — commit: h[j] = 1, h[i] = 0; Tplus += dS − d; Tminus += dP − d; S[] update? Via dS ✓ S not separate (T array already maintained). bestT = cand.

Arrays: Tplus, Tminus over k = 1..n−1 (length n index 0..n−1, unused 0). dS, dP similarly.

Evaluation: C5 = bestT·(2/n).

Full-sweep design:
For each i with h[i] = 1 (list_pos1), for each j with h[j] = 0 (l0), j > i (j < i symmetric standard evaluation): cand (swap i), if better commit and update lists (pair order recompute (restart at top of loop, cheap)). Cost: per-pair ~15-25μs (delay some dS/dP): n=160 16k pairs ≈ 0.3-0.5 s ✓.

Completion sweep; then "second order": swap 1st is unprofitable but 2nd follow-up (A→B→C) can profit: multi-flip: random 2 pairs: apply and apply? Move enumeration: pick 2 ones i1,i2 and 2 zeros j1,j2 gross evaluation: simple: i1→j1 apply δF1, i2→j2 δF2 (order); total ΔF = d1+d2 (+corrections), ΔS/ΔP: max improvement? Step: from state, random double-move trial (4-tuple), accept if improvement; iterate ~20k times. Cheaper: 3-flip-style "slide": in a row 1 0 → 0 1 (move 1 to adjacent 0) is adjacent swap i, i+1 in "binary" rearrangement — this is a permutational T transformation! Traverse = T-hamiltonian: all compositions are reachable by adjacent swaps (adjacent 1↔0). Each adjacent swap is a simple dF (k = 1 interval?) with |i−j| = 1, k = 1? δ correction only. More simple curve: value-to-value dF shift: values themselves? From left-to-right final pass of T-array (with twists): adjacent swap 10 → 01: more distinct, more? Fine: maintain numpy (already around). 3-opt "arrangement" optional.

Exhaustive implementation detail:

def exhaustive_small(n, time_cap):
    k = n//2
    bestA = None; bestT = float('inf')
    from itertools import combinations
    t0 = time.time()
    idxs = list(range(n))
    for combo in combinations(idxs, k):
        A = 0
        for p in combo: A |= (1 << p)
        T = eval_A(n, A)
        if T < bestT: bestT = T; bestA = A
        if len? completion: if (n not in (12,14,16,18,20,22)) and time.time()−t0 > time_cap: break
    return bestA, bestT
elif n in (24, 26, 28): same, cap.

So generally: ΔS: −1 on (b, a] (if b < a) & +1 on (a, b] (if a < b): unify: ΔS(k) = +1 if... (min + 1..max)-sign. def exhaustive_search(n, time_cap=30):
    k = n//2; bestT = inf; bestA = 0
    t0 = time.time()
    for combo in itertools.combinations(range(n), k):
        A = 0; for p in combo: A |= 1 << p
        T = eval_A(n, A)
        if T < bestT: bestT = T; bestA = A
        if combo count % 50000 == 0: if time.time() − t0 > time_cap: return (bestA, bestT, False)
    return (bestA, bestT, True)

```python
import time
import numpy as np

def clip_h(h):
    return np.clip(np.asarray(h, dtype=float), 0.0, 1.0)

def fix_sum(h, n):
    h = h.copy()
    target = 0.5 * n
    for _ in range(6):
        d = target - h.sum()
        if abs(d) < 1e-12: break
        m = (h > 1e-9) & (h < 1.0 - 1e-9)
        c = m.sum()
        if c > 0: h[m] += d / c
        else:
            if d > 0:
                order = np.argsort(-h); h[order[0]] += d
            else:
                order = np.argsort(h); h[order[0]] -= d
        np.clip(h, 0, 1, out=h)
    return h

def snap_binary(h, n):
    r = np.round(h)
    r = np.clip(r, 0.0, 1.0)
    need = 0.5 * n - r.sum()
    if need > 1e-9:
        cand = np.nonzero(r < 1.0)[0]
        order = np.argsort(-(0.5 - h[cand]))  # closest to 1 first? To 1: h large
        # choose smallest (0.5 - h)? Largest h (closest to 1)
        order = np.argsort(-h[cand])
        for t in order[:max(1, int(need))]:
            r[cand[t]] = 1.0
    elif need < -1e-9:
        cand = np.nonzero(r > 0.0)[0]
        order = np.argsort(h[cand])  # smallest = closest to 0
        r[cand[:max(1, -int(need))]] = 0.0
    return r
```
h[cand] float OK.

```python
def init_tm(n):
    m = max(1, int(np.ceil(np.log2(n))))
    h = np.empty(n)
    for i in range(n):
        b = (i << m)
        # parity of popcount
        cnt = bin(b & ((1 << 60) - 1)).count("1") & 1   # n small
        # tm(i) = popcount(i)&1; tm(i<<m) = popcount(i)&1 ^ (m&1)
        h[i] = 1.0 if ((bin(i).count("1") & 1) ^ (m & 1)) else 0.0
    return h
```
(h[i] = 1 at parity: sum = n/2? parity balanced on 2^m blocks ✓ n multiple 2^m) ✓
(Speed: n few k × bin string — fine; or bit op loop.)

Simpler:
```
def _pop_parity(x):
    x ^= x >> 32; x ^= x >> 16; x ^= x >> 8; x ^= x >> 4; x &= 0xf; return (0x6996 >> x) & 1
```

```python
def init_wave(n, alpha):
    t = np.arange(n)
    return (np.sin(2*np.pi*alpha*t) > 0).astype(float)
```

```python
def init_gold(n, rng):
    phi = (1 + 5**0.5)/4
    t = np.arange(n)
    x = (t * phi) % 1
    h = (x > 0.5).astype(float)
    need = n / 2 - h.sum()
    if need != 0:
        cand = np.nonzero(1 - h)[0]
        order = np.argsort(-(0.5 - x[cand]))  # x closest to 0.5
        for i in order[:int(abs(need))]:
            h[cand[i]] = 1.0 if need > 0 else 0.0
        if need > 0:
            zero outside? to 1 count needed: pos need; if need < 0 pos need
    return h
```

```python
def full_sweep(n, h, Tp, Tm, rng, max_sweeps, tstop):
    npr = 0.5 * n
    imp = 0
    for sw in range(max_sweeps):
        if time.time() > tstop: break
        ones = np.nonzero(h > 0.5)[0]
        zeros = np.nonzero(h < 0.5)[0]
        order = rng.permutation(len(ones))
        anyim = False
        for ii in order:
            a = ones[ii]
            for b in zeros:
                if b == a: continue
                cm, ok = eval_swap(n, h, Tp, Tm, a, b, None)
                if ok and cm < curmax - 1e-12:
                    # apply
                    ...
                    curmax = cm; anyim = True; imp += 1
                    ones = ...; zeros = ...  # recompute (cheap)
        if not anyim: break
    return h, Tp, Tm, imp
```

```python
def swap_delta(n, h, a, b, W):
    # W: dictionary-length scratch W1..W6
    s = 1.0 if b > a else -1.0
    lo = a if b > a else b
    hi = b if b > a else a
    dS = W["dS"]; dP = W["dP"]; dS.fill(0.0); dP.fill(0.0)
    dS[lo+1: hi+1] = s
    dP[n-hi + 1: n-lo + 1] = -s  # verify: b<a (s=-1): interval k = n-a + 1.. n-b: hi = a, lo = b: n-hi+1 = n-a+1, n-lo+1 = n-b+1 exclusive → indices n-a+1.. n-b ✓
    D1 = W["D1"]; D2 = W["D2"]; A1 = W["A1"]; A2 = W["A2"]
    D1.fill(0.0); D2.fill(0.0); A1.fill(0.0); A2.fill(0.0)
    la = n - 1 - a
    if la >= 1: A1[1: la+1] = -h[a+1: n]
    if a >= 1: A2[1: a+1] = -h[a-1::-1]
    lb = n - 1 - b
    if lb >= 1: D1[1: lb+1] = h[b+1: n]
    if b >= 1: D2[1: b+1] = h[b-1::-1]
    # DF = D1 + D2 - A1abs...: DF = D1 + D2; DF -= A1? sign: A is subtract h; ΔF = D1 + D2 + A1 + A2 (A is negative): DF = (D1 + D2) + (A1 + A2): W DF = df:
    DF = W["DF"]; np.add(D1, D2, out=DF); np.add(DF, A1, out=DF); np.add(DF, A2, out=DF)
    if hi - lo >= 1:
        DF[hi - lo] -= 1.0
    return dS, dP, DF, s
```

```python
def polish_pair(n, h, Tp, Tm, rng, rounds=2, tstop=0, gridN=9):
    # 2-point (a: 0, b: 1... no: pair (a 1→1−t, b 0→t)? Sum preservation: (t, 1−t): 0 → t, 1 → 1−t: net 0 ✓. Sample all (a 0, b 1) pair (n²/4) too many (n=512 65k) × 9 t × cost (2 lags ~2n·? update n max (mul-add 2×(n) + max 2) ~ 8μs) → 65k·9·8μs = 4.7 s/round OK; n ≥ 512 round risk cap.
    tgrid = np.linspace(0.1, 0.9, 9)
    for rd in range(rounds):
        if time.time() > tstop: break
        zs = np.nonzero(h < 1e-9)[0]
        os_ = np.nonzero(h > 1 - 1e-9)[0]  # Need h exactly 0/1 (binary state) for linear coefficient!
        # if h decimal (non-binary) skip (as binary state required)
        improved = False
        order...
        for b in zs:

def candidate_value(h):
    n = len(h)
    c = np.max(np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full")) * (2.0 / n)
    return c, n

Simple: for best candidate clip & sum-fix, except weak proj:
```
def proj_sum(h, n):
    h = np.clip(np.asarray(h, dtype=float), 0.0, 1.0).copy()
    target = 0.5 * n
    for _ in range(8):
        d = target - h.sum()
        if abs(d) < 1e-12: break
        m = (h > 1e-7) & (h < 1.0 - 1e-7)
        if m.any():
            h[m] += d / m.sum()
        elif d > 0:
            o = np.argsort(-h); h[o[0]] += d
        else:
            o = np.argsort(h); h[o[0]] += d
        np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0, out=h)
    return h
```

def full_sweep(n, h, Tp, Tm, rng, iters_max, tstop):
    W = dict of length n arrays
    cur = max float(Tp.max(), Tm.max())
    improved_total = 0
    for it in range(iters_max):
        if time.time() > tstop: break
        ones = np.nonzero(h > 0.5)[0]
        zeros = np.nonzero(h < 0.5)[0]
        imp = 0
        for a in ones:
            improved_this_a = False
            for b in zeros:
                if b == a: continue
                # compute candidate
                (compute & decide)
                if apply: h[b]=1; h[a]=0; cur = cm; imp += 1; improved_this_a = True (resume b loop? Old l list; zero list remove to... recompute: jump out for, recompute & break a-loop? To outer? Python: local "break outer" flag.)
        if imp == 0: break
        improved_total += imp
    return h, Tp, Tm, improved_total
```

def verify_T(n, h, Tp, Tm):
    c = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full")
    Tpf = c[n-1:]  # k = 0..n−1: c[n−1+k]
    Tmf = c[n-1::-1] # c[n−1−k], k = 0...: k = n−1..0
    return np.abs(Tp - Tpf[:n]).max(), np.abs(Tm - Tmf[::-1][:n]).max()
    # Tmf index: k = 0..n−1, c[n−1−k]: k = n−1 → c[0]: array c[n−1::-1] → k = 0..n−1 ✓ length n ✓

def run(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    t0 = time.time()
    try: budget = float(budget_s)
    except: budget = 1300.0
    budget = max(30.0, budget)
    tEND = t0 + budget - 4.0
    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)
    bestC5 = float("inf"); bestH = None; bestN = 0
    parent = None
    try:

        if p.ndim == 1 and p.size >= 8 and np.all(np.isfinite(p)):
            parent = p
    except Exception:

def full_sweep(n, h, Tp, Tm, rnd, iters, tstop):
    WdS = np.zeros(n); WdP = np.zeros(n); WDF = np.zeros(n); WS = np.zeros(n); WT = np.zeros(n)
    cur = max(float(Tp.max()), float(Tm.max()))
    impT = 0
    it = 0
    while it < iters and time.time() < tstop:
        it += 1
        ones = np.nonzero(h >= 0.5)[0]
        zeros = np.nonzero(h < 0.5)[0]
        changed = 0
        order = rnd.permutation(len(ones))
        for oi in order:
            a = int(ones[oi])
            if h[a] < 0.5: continue  # changed after prior (old)
            im = False
            for b in zeros:
                b = int(b)
                if b == a or h[b] >= 0.5 or h[a] < 0.5: continue
                s = 1.0 if b > a else -1.0
                lo = a if a < b else b; hi = b if a < b else a
                WdS.fill(0.0); WdS[lo+1:hi+1] = s
                WdP.fill(0.0); WdP[n-hi+1:n-lo+1] = -s
                WDF.fill(0.0)
                la = n-1-a
                if la >= 1: WDF[1:la+1] += -h[a+1:a+la+1]  # k = 1..la: h[a+1..a+la] (a+la = n−1 ✓)
                if a >= 1: WDF[1:a+1] += -h[a-1::-1]
                lb = n-1-b
                if lb >= 1: WDF[1:lb+1] += h[b+1:b+lb+1]
                if b >= 1: WDF[1:b+1] += h[b-1::-1]
                dk = hi-lo  # ≥ 1 (a≠b)
                WDF[dk] -= 1.0
                np.subtract(WdS, WDF, out=WS)
                np.add(Tp, WS, out=WT); m1 = float(WT.max())
                np.subtract(WdP, WDF, out=WS)
                np.add(Tm, WS, out=WT); m2 = float(WT.max())
                cm = m1 if m1 > m2 else m2
                if cm < cur - 1e-12:
                    np.add(Tp, WS, out=Tp)
                    np.subtract(WdP, WDF, out=WS)
                    np.add(Tm, WS, out=Tm)
                    h[a] = 0.0; h[b] = 1.0
                    cur = cm; changed += 1; im = True; break
        if changed == 0: break
        impT += changed
    return h, Tp, Tm, cur, impT

Apply update: h: a = 1→0, b = 0→1: h[a] = 0, h[b] = 1 ✓ (a is 1, b is 0 valid verified).

def polish_round(n, h, Tp, Tm, rnd, tstop, grid):
    # Grin king round each index vector
    # return (h, Tp, Tm, imp)
    # Cplus[p][k] = [k ≤ p] − h[p+k]·1{p+k<n} − h[p−k]·1{p−k≥0}
    idx = np.arange(1, n)
    P = np.arange(n)
    M = idx[None, :]  # (n, n-1)
    mk1 = (P[:, None] + M < n)
    H1 = np.where(mk1, h[(P[:, None] + M) % n] — careful: fancy index n×(n−1) = 256 k F (n=512) 262k — OK cache.
    Actually: idxf1 = (P[:, None] + M); H1(n, m) = np.where(mk1, h[idxf1[...? no cost guard (x, -1), INF → large skip (x, -1) mask → large skip (M*e.))
    Compute: A = P[:, None] + M; B = P[:, None] − M
    H1 = np.where(A < n, h[np.where(A < n, A, 0)], 0.0)
    H2 = np.where(B >= 0, h[np.where(B >= 0, B, 0)], 0.0)
    Cplus = (M <= P[:, None]).astype(float) − H1 − H2  # (n, n−1)
    Cminus0 (M <= n−1−P[:, None]) minus same H1, H2
    Memory n=512: 512×511×8 = 2.1MB × few cache ✓ time ~ 50ms per round OK.

def build_T(n, h):
    g = 1.0 − h
    c = np.correlate(h, g, mode="full")
    # c index 0..2n−2; after: lag k (1..n−1), plus = c[n-1+k]
    Tp = np.zeros(n); Tm = np.zeros(n)
    Tp[1:] = c[n:2*n-1]  # k=1..n-1 → index n..2n-2 ✓ length n-1
    # minus: c[n-1-k] (k=1..n−1) = (n−2)..0
    Tm[1:] = c[n-2::-1]  # c[n−2], c[n−3],... c[0] for k=1..n−1 ✓
    return Tp, Tm

Exhaustive encode:
def eval_bit(n, A):
    lower = 0
    for k in range(1, n):
        F = (A & (A >> k)).bit_count()
        S = 0
        # S(k) = h sum for k..n−1: bit ≈ A & (~((1<<k)−1))
        S = (A & (full_mask ^ ((1 << k) − 1))).bit_count()
        pt = S − F
        P = (A & ((1 << (n − k)) − 1)).bit_count()
        pm = P − F
        if pt > lower: lower = pt
        if pm > lower: lower = pm
    return lower'
full_mask = (1 << n) − 1 (n ≤ 26 OK).

Final code structure:
```python
import time
import numpy as np

_TARGET = 0.38085

def clip_hd(h):
    np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0, out=h)
    return h

def proj_sum(h, n):
    h = np.array(h, dtype=float)
    np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0, out=h)
    target = 0.5 * n
    for _ in range(10):
        d = target - h.sum()
        if abs(d) < 1e-12:
            break
        m = (h > 1e-7) & (h < 1.0 - 1e-7)
        c = int(m.sum())
        if c > 0:
            h[m] += d / c
        elif d > 0:
            o = int(np.argmax(h)); h[o] = 1.0  # max move: max h + small
            # add d to o: h[o] may > 1: re-clip
        else:
            o = int(np.argmin(h))
            h[o] = h[o] + d
        np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0, out=h)
    return h
```
Wait: if all near 0/1 & d > 0: h[o] set to 1 adds 1−h[o] (> 0) & d may not be exactly: iterate 10 times enough for parent-like (parent fractional enough).

```python
def snap3(h, rnd):
    n = len(h)
    order = np.argsort(np.abs(np.asarray(h, dtype=float) - 0.5))
    r = np.zeros(n)
    for t in order:
        r[t] = 1.0 if h[t] >= 0.5 else 0.0
    have = float(r.sum())
    need = int(round(0.5 * n - have))
    if need > 0:
        zc = np.nonzero(r == 0.0)[0]
        for t in zc[:need]:
            r[t] = 1.0
    elif need < 0:
        oc = np.nonzero(r == 1.0)[0]
        for t in oc[:(-need)]:
            r[t] = 0.0
    return r
```
(Deterministic pre-sort: OK; or rnd permute — using order: OK.)

c5 value:
```python
def c5_value(h):
    n = len(h)
    return float(np.max(np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full"))) * (2.0 / n)
```

Init:
```python
def init_tm(n):
    m = 0
    while (1 << m) < n:
        m += 1
    h = np.zeros(n)
    for i in range(n):
        # n multiple 2^m? No: par rp: period 2^m on t within [0,n)?
        # Define t index within [0,n): position shift p = i·? no
        # Choice: T = i//? no.
        # Simplest: h_i = tm_bit(i·2^m) prior D or (tm parity or (m odd)) as above.
        pc = 0
        v = i
        while v:
            pc ^= v & 1
            v >>= 1
        pc ^= (m & 1)
        h[i] = 1.0 if pc == 1 else 0.0
    return h
```

```python
def init_gold(n, rng):
    ph = (1.0 + np.sqrt(5.0)) / 4.0
    t = (np.arange(n) * ph + rng.rand() * ph) % 1.0
    h = (t > 0.5).astype(float)
    need = int(round(0.5*n - h.sum()))
    if need > 0:
        zc = np.nonzero(h < 1.0)[0]
        order = np.argsort(-(0.5 - t[zc].clip(0, None)))
```
Simply: (select t-zero closest to 0.5 & highest), 1 → need count; need < 0 similarly low→0.

def init_ran(n, r):
    p = r.permutation(n)
    h = np.zeros(n); h[p[: n//2]] = 1.0
    return h

T build & verify:
def build_T(n, h):
    c = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full")
    T = np.zeros(n); Ts = np.zeros(n)
    T[1:] = c[n: 2*n - 1]
    Ts[1:] = c[n - 2::-1]
    return T, Ts
```
T(m=0) = c[n−1] both sides match ✓ setting; no-op (& 0/1 = 0).

full_sweep (as above):
```python
def full_sweep(n, h, T, Ts, r, iters, tstop):
    cd = np.zeros(n); cp = np.zeros(n); cf = np.zeros(n); ws = np.zeros(n); wt = np.zeros(n)
    cur = max(float(T.max()), float(Ts.max()))
    impT = 0
    it = 0
    improved = True
    while it < iters and improved and time.time() < tstop:
        it += 1
        improved = False
        ones = np.nonzero(h >= 0.5)[0]
        zeros = np.nonzero(h < 0.5)[0]
        for a in r.permutation(ones):
            if h[a] < 0.5: continue
            for b in zeros:
                if b == a or h[b] >= 0.5: continue
                # a:1→0, b:0→1
                s = 1.0 if b > a else -1.0
                lo = a if a < b else b
                hi = b if a < b else a
                cd.fill(0.0); cd[lo + 1:hi + 1] = s
                cp.fill(0.0); cp[n - hi + 1:n - lo + 1] = -s
                cf.fill(0.0)
                la = n - 1 - a
                if la >= 1: cf[1:la + 1] += -h[a + 1:a + la + 1]
                if a >= 1: cf[1:a + 1] += -h[a - 1::-1]
                lb = n - 1 - b
                if lb >= 1: cf[1:lb + 1] += h[b + 1:b + lb + 1]
                if b >= 1: cf[1:b + 1] += h[b - 1::-1]
                if hi - lo >= 1: cf[hi - lo] -= 1.0
                np.subtract(cd, cf, out=ws); np.add(T, ws, out=wt); m1 = float(wt.max())
                np.subtract(cp, cf, out=ws); np.add(Ts, ws, out=wt); m2 = float(wt.max())
                cm = m1 if m1 > m2 else m2
                if cm < cur - 1e-12:
                    np.add(T, ws, out=T)
                    np.subtract(cp, cf, out=ws); np.add(Ts, ws, out=Ts)
                    h[a] = 0.0; h[b] = 1.0
                    cur = cm
                    improved = True
                    break
        # local improvement → next `while`
    return h, T, Ts, cur, ...
```
Note: `break` stops `b` loop after improvement; then consecutive a with old-lists & zero list (old b list, valid by `h` valid). a = 0 changed skip (`h` valid) OK. `zeros` list recompute (per `while`) & stale & skip by `h` valid ✓. Complete & OK.

Hmm: pair skips `b` (b = 1 setting in & improve; subsequent a's b = 1? b is remain 0 — OK; pair `a` old a = 0: `if h[a] < 0.5: continue` valid ✓.

```python
def polish_decomp(n, h, T, Ts, r, rounds, tstop):
    # maintain T & Ts, h state (fractional content or 0/1)
    # For each index pair (i from high, j from low, possible singular), try t values preserving v_i', v_j': v_i + v_j invariant:
    # Prefer i in [0.8,1] & j in [0,0.2], then rest.
    for rd in range(rounds):
        if time.time() > tstop: break
        hi_idx = np.nonzero(h > 0.5)[0]
        lo_idx = np.nonzero(h <= 0.5)[0]
        changed = False
        for i in r.permutation(hi_idx):
            vi = h[i]
            for j in lo_idx:  # all low ~ n/2 → limited (first 40 low in seed order)
                vj = h[j]
                s = vi + vj
                # t candidate: for u in [0..1]: v_i' = s-u, v_j' = u requires 0 ≤ s−u ≤ 1, 0 ≤ u ≤ 1: u ∈ [max(0, s−1), min(1, s)]
                for u in (0.0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0):
                    if u < max(0.0, s - 1.0) - 1e-12 or u > min(1.0, s) + 1e-12: continue
                    ... compute candidate via slope ...
        if not changed: break
    return h, T, Ts
```

Frame correction:

```python
def init_bal(n, shift):
    h = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    half = n // 2
    for i in range(half):
        p = int(np.round((2*i + 1 + 2*shift) * n / (2.0*half))) % n
        if h[p] == 0.0:
            h[p] = 1.0
        else:
            h[p+1] = 1.0 if h[p+1] == 0.0 else h[p-1] = 1.0
```
Pseudo — focused correct:

```python
def init_bal(n, shift):
    h = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    half = n//2
    cnt = 0
    i = 0
    while cnt < half:
        p = int(round(((2*i + 1) + 2*shift) * n / (2.0*half))) % n
        if h[p] < 0.5:
            h[p] = 1.0
            cnt += 1
        i += 1
    return h
```

golden:
```python
def init_gold(n, rr):
    phi = (1.0 + np.sqrt(5.0)) * 0.25
    x = (np.arange(n) * phi + rr.random()) % 1.0
    h = (x < 0.5).astype(float)
    need = n//2 - int(h.sum())
    if need > 0:
        order = np.argsort(-x)
        for t in order:
            if need == 0: break
            if h[t] == 0.0: h[t] = 1.0; need -= 1
    elif need < 0:
        order = np.argsort(x)
        need = -need
        for t in order:
            if need == 0: break
            if h[t] == 1.0: h[t] = 0.0; need -= 1
    return h
```

initlerp(n, ph, rnd):
```python
def initlerp(n, ph, rnd):
    m = len(ph)
    if m == n:
        h = snap3(ph copy, rnd)  → & & interp = ph copy
        return h
    xp = (np.arange(m) + 0.5) * 2.0 / m
    xn = (np.arange(n) + 0.5) * 2.0 / n
    h = np.interp(xn, xp, ph)
    return snap3(h, rnd)
```

```python
def search_n(n, parent, r, time_budget, tstop):
    starts = []
    starts.append(init_tm(n))
    for sh in (0, 1, 2):
        starts.append(init_bal(n, sh))
    starts.append(init_gold(n, r))
    starts.append(init_ran(n, r))
    if parent is not None and len(parent) >= n//2 and len(parent) <= n*2:
        starts.append(initlerp(n, parent, r))
    startbest = float("inf")
    best = None
    for h0 in starts:
        if time.time() > tstop or time.time() > t_n: break
        h = h0.copy(); T, Ts = buildT(n, h)
        full_sweep(n, h, T, Ts, r, iters=default, tstop=n_t)
        if n <= 256: amp(n, h, T, Ts, r, 1, tstop=n_t)
        if n <= 300: polish(n, h, T, Ts, r, 2, tstop=n_t)
        # if decimal candidate record: value & sum check
        c = c5value(h)
        if abs(h.sum() - n/2) <= 1e-9 and np.all(h >= 0) ...: record candidate: & T arrays & ll corr verify
    return best

```python
import time
import numpy as np

# ---------------- helpers ----------------

def clip_unit(h):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    return np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)


def proj_sum(h, n):
    h = clip_unit(h).copy()
    target = 0.5 * n
    for _ in range(12):
        d = target - h.sum()
        if abs(d) < 1e-12:
            break
        m = (h > 1e-7) & (h < 1.0 - 1e-7)
        c = int(m.sum())
        if c > 0:
            h[m] += d / c
        elif d > 0:
            o = int(np.argmax(h))
            h[o] = 1.0
        else:
            o = int(np.argmin(h))
            h[o] = 0.0
        np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0, out=h)
    return h


def snap3(h):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    n = len(h)
    order = np.argsort(np.abs(h - 0.5))
    r = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    for t in order:
        if h[t] >= 0.5:
            r[t] = 1.0
    have = float(r.sum())
    need = int(round(0.5 * n - have))
    if need > 0:
        zc = np.nonzero(r == 0.0)[0]
        for t in zc[: max(0, need)]:
            r[t] = 1.0
    elif need < 0:
        oc = np.nonzero(r == 1.0)[0]
        for t in oc[: max(0, -need)]:
            r[t] = 0.0
    return r


def c5_value(h):
    n = len(h)
    c = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full")
    return float(np.max(c)) * (2.0 / n)


def init_tm(n):
    m = 0
    while (1 << m) < n:
        m += 1
    h = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    for i in range(n):
        pc = 0
        v = i
        while v:
            pc ^= (v & 1)
            v >>= 1
        pc ^= (m & 1)
        h[i] = 1.0 if pc == 1 else 0.0
    d = 0.5 * n - float(h.sum())
    if d != 0:
        h = _fix_count(h, int(round(d)))
    return h


def _fix_count(h, d, snap_zero=True):
    n = len(h)
    for _ in range(abs(d) + 2):
        if d > 0:
            z = np.nonzero(h < 0.5)[0]
            if z.size == 0:
                break
            t = z[np.argmax(h[z])]
            h[t] = 1.0
            d -= 1
        elif d < 0:
            o = np.nonzero(h > 0.5)[0]
            if o.size == 0:
                break
            t = o[np.argmin(h[o])]
            h[t] = 0.0
            d += 1
        else:
            break
    if snap_zero:
        h = np.where(h > 0.5, 1.0, 0.0)
    return h


def init_bal(n, shift):
    h = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    half = n // 2
    cnt = 0
    i = 0
    limit = 8 * n
    while cnt < half and i < limit:
        p = int(round((2.0 * i + 1.0 + 2.0 * shift) * n / (2.0 * half))) % n
        if h[p] < 0.5:
            h[p] = 1.0
            cnt += 1
        i += 1
    while cnt < half:
        done = True
        for p in range(n):
            if h[p] < 0.5:
                h[p] = 1.0
                cnt += 1
                done = False
                break
        if done:
            break
    return h


def init_gold(n, rnd):
    phi = (1.0 + np.sqrt(5.0)) * 0.25
    x = (np.arange(n) * phi + rnd.random()) % 1.0
    h = (x < 0.5).astype(float)
    d = n // 2 - int(h.sum())
    h = _fix_count(h, d)
    return h


def init_ran(n, rnd):
    p = rnd.permutation(n)
    h = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    h[p[: n // 2]] = 1.0
    return h


def initlerp(n, ph, rnd):
    m = len(ph)
    if m == n:
        return snap3(clip_unit(ph))
    xp = (np.arange(m) + 0.5) * (2.0 / m)
    xn = (np.arange(n) + 0.5) * (2.0 / n)
    h = np.interp(xn, xp, clip_unit(ph))
    return snap3(h)


def buildT(n, h):
    c = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full")
    Tp = np.zeros(n)
    Tm = np.zeros(n)
    Tp[1:] = c[n: 2 * n - 1]
    Tm[1:] = c[n - 2::-1]
    return Tp, Tm


def verifyT(n, h, Tp, Tm):
    c = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full")
    Tpf = np.zeros(n)
    Tmf = np.zeros(n)
    Tpf[1:] = c[n: 2 * n - 1]
    Tmf[1:] = c[n - 2::-1]
    return max(float(np.max(np.abs(Tp - Tpf))), float(np.max(np.abs(Tm - Tmf))))


def full_sweep(n, h, Tp, Tm, r, iters, tstop):
    cd = np.zeros(n)
    cp = np.zeros(n)
    cf = np.zeros(n)
    ws = np.zeros(n)
    wt = np.zeros(n)
    cur = max(float(Tp.max()), float(Tm.max()))
    it = 0
    while it < iters and time.time() < tstop:
        improved = False
        it += 1
        ones = np.nonzero(h >= 0.5)[0]
        zeros = np.nonzero(h < 0.5)[0]
        for a in r.permutation(ones):
            a = int(a)
            if h[a] < 0.5:
                continue
            for b in zeros:
                b = int(b)
                if b == a or h[b] >= 0.5:
                    continue
                sgn = 1.0 if b > a else -1.0
                lo = a if a < b else b
                hi = b if a < b else a
                cd.fill(0.0)
                cd[lo + 1:hi + 1] = sgn
                cp.fill(0.0)
                cp[n - hi + 1:n - lo + 1] = -sgn
                cf.fill(0.0)
                la = n - 1 - a
                if la >= 1:
                    cf[1:la + 1] += -h[a + 1:a + la + 1]
                if a >= 1:
                    cf[1:a + 1] += -h[a - 1::-1]
                lb = n - 1 - b
                if lb >= 1:
                    cf[1:lb + 1] += h[b + 1:b + lb + 1]
                if b >= 1:
                    cf[1:b + 1] += h[b - 1::-1]
                dk = hi - lo
                cf[dk] -= 1.0
                np.subtract(cd, cf, out=ws)
                np.add(Tp, ws, out=wt)
                m1 = float(wt.max())
                np.subtract(cp, cf, out=ws)
                np.add(Tm, ws, out=wt)
                m2 = float(wt.max())
                cm = m1 if m1 > m2 else m2
                if cm < cur - 1e-12:
                    np.add(Tp, ws, out=Tp)
                    np.subtract(cp, cf, out=ws)
                    np.add(Tm, ws, out=Tm)
                    h[a] = 0.0
                    h[b] = 1.0
                    cur = cm
                    zeros = np.nonzero(h < 0.5)[0]
                    improved = True
                    break
        if not improved:
            break
    return cur


def attempt_uphill(n, h, Tp, Tm, r, tstop):
    cd = np.zeros(n)
    cp = np.zeros(n)
    cf = np.zeros(n)
    ws = np.zeros(n)
    wt = np.zeros(n)
    cur = max(float(Tp.max()), float(Tm.max()))
    ones = list(np.nonzero(h >= 0.5)[0])
    zeros = list(np.nonzero(h < 0.5)[0])
    for _ in range(300):
        if time.time() > tstop:
            break
        if len(ones) == 0 or len(zeros) == 0:
            break
        a = ones[r.randint(len(ones))]
        b = zeros[r.randint(len(zeros))]
        if a == b:
            continue
        sgn = 1.0 if b > a else -1.0
        lo = a if a < b else b
        hi = b if a < b else a
        cd.fill(0.0)
        cd[lo + 1:hi + 1] = sgn
        cp.fill(0.0)
        cp[n - hi + 1:n - lo + 1] = -sgn
        cf.fill(0.0)
        la = n - 1 - a
        if la >= 1:
            cf[1:la + 1] += -h[a + 1:a + la + 1]
        if a >= 1:
            cf[1:a + 1] += -h[a - 1::-1]
        lb = n - 1 - b
        if lb >= 1:
            cf[1:lb + 1] += h[b + 1:b + lb + 1]
        if b >= 1:
            cf[1:b + 1] += h[b - 1::-1]
        dk = hi - lo
        cf[dk] -= 1.0
        np.subtract(cd, cf, out=ws)
        np.add(Tp, ws, out=wt)
        m1 = float(wt.max())
        np.subtract(cp, cf, out=ws)
        np.add(Tm, ws, out=wt)
        m2 = float(wt.max())
        cm = m1 if m1 > m2 else m2
        if cm < cur * 1.05 + 2.0:
            np.add(Tp, ws, out=Tp)
            np.subtract(cp, cf, out=ws)
            np.add(Tm, ws, out=Tm)
            h[a] = 0.0
            h[b] = 1.0
            cur = cm
            ones.remove(a)
            zeros.remove(b)
            zeros.append(a)
            ones.append(b)
    return cur


def polish_round(n, h, Tp, Tm, r, tstop, jmax=26):
    hih = list(np.nonzero(h > 0.5)[0])
    loh = list(np.nonzero(h < 0.5)[0])
    if len(hih) == 0 or len(loh) == 0:
        return
    ws1 = np.zeros(n)
    ws2 = np.zeros(n)
    wt1 = np.zeros(n)
    wt2 = np.zeros(n)
    cpi = np.zeros(n)
    csi = np.zeros(n)
    wgt = np.zeros(n)
    wgt2 = np.zeros(n)
    step = np.zeros(n)
    ugrid = np.round(np.arange(1, 16, 1, dtype=float) / 16.0, 2)
    anyim = False
    for ii in r.permutation(len(hih)):
        if time.time() > tstop:
            return
        i = hih[ii]
        if h[i] < 0.5:
            continue
        step.fill(0.0)
        step[1:i + 1] = 1.0
        np.copyto(cpi, step)
        wgt.fill(0.0)
        if i + 1 <= n - 1:
            wgt[1:n - i] = h[i + 1:n]
        cpi -= wgt
        wgt2.fill(0.0)
        if i >= 1:
            wgt2[1:i + 1] = h[i - 1::-1]
        cpi -= wgt2
        csi.fill(0.0)
        csi[1:n - i] = 1.0
        csi -= wgt
        csi -= wgt2
        vi = float(h[i])
        sub = r.permutation(len(loh))[:jmax]
        for jj in sub:
            j = loh[jj]
            if h[j] >= 0.5:
                continue
            vj = float(h[j])
            cpij = np.subtract(cpi, np.zeros(1))
            step.fill(0.0)
            step[1:j + 1] = 1.0
            cpij = step.copy()
            wgt.fill(0.0)
            if j + 1 <= n - 1:
                wgt[1:n - j] = h[j + 1:n]
            cpij -= wgt
            wgt2.fill(0.0)
            if j >= 1:
                wgt2[1:j + 1] = h[j - 1::-1]
            cpij -= wgt2
            csij = step.copy()
            csij.fill(0.0)
            csij[1:n - j] = 1.0
            wgt.fill(0.0)
            if j + 1 <= n - 1:
                wgt[1:n - j] = h[j + 1:n]
            csij -= wgt
            csij -= wgt2
            dtp = cpij - cpi
            dtm = csij - csi
            mu = max(0.0, vi + vj - 1.0)
            Mup = min(1.0, vi + vj)
            bestu = None
            bestv = max(float(Tp.max()), float(Tm.max()))
            for u in ugrid:
                if u < mu - 1e-9 or u > Mup + 1e-9:
                    continue
                if abs(u - vj) < 0.01 and abs(u - 1.0 + (vi + vj - 1.0)) > 0.99:
                    pass
                np.multiply(dtp, u - vj, out=ws1)
                np.multiply(dtm, u - vj, out=ws2)
                np.add(Tp, ws1, out=wt1)
            ma = float(wt1.max())
            return  # placeholder never reached


def polish(n, h, Tp, Tm, r, rounds, tstop):
    dxv = 2.0 / n
    for rr in range(rounds):
        if time.time() > tstop:
            break
        hih = list(np.nonzero(h > 0.5)[0])
        loh = list(np.nonzero(h < 0.5)[0])
        if not hih or not loh:
            break
        anyim = False
        for ii in r.permutation(len(hih)):
            if time.time() > tstop:
                return
            i = int(hih[ii])
            if h[i] < 0.5:
                continue
            vi = float(h[i])
            # slopes for index i
            K = np.arange(1, n)
            cp1 = (K <= i).astype(float)
            cm1 = (K <= n - 1 - i).astype(float)
            fterm = np.zeros(n - 1)
            if i + 1 <= n - 1:
                fterm[:n - 1 - i] = h[i + 1:n]
            if i >= 1:
                fterm[:i] = h[i - 1::-1]
            cp1 -= fterm
            cm1 -= fterm
            nsub = min(30, len(loh))
            order = r.permutation(len(loh))[:nsub]
            for jj in order:
                j = int(loh[jj])
                if h[j] >= 0.5 or j == i:
                    continue
                vj = float(h[j])
                cp2 = (K <= j).astype(float)
                cm2 = (K <= n - 1 - j).astype(float)
                fterm2 = np.zeros(n - 1)
                if j + 1 <= n - 1:
                    fterm2[:n - 1 - j] = h[j + 1:n]
                if j >= 1:
                    fterm2[:j] = h[j - 1::-1]
                cp2 -= fterm2
                cm2 -= fterm2
                dtp = cp2 - cp1
                dtm = cm2 - cm1
                # T(0 part): v_i'=(s-u), v_j'=u, s=vi+vj
                s = vi + vj
                base0 = float(Tp[0]) if n > 0 else 0.0
                t0rest = base0 - vi * (1.0 - vi) - vj * (1.0 - vj)
                ugrid = np.array([0.0, 0.0625, 0.125, 0.1875, 0.25, 0.3125, 0.375,
                                  0.4375, 0.5, 0.5625, 0.625, 0.6875, 0.75,
                                  0.8125, 0.875, 0.9375, 1.0])
                lo = max(0.0, s - 1.0)
                hi = min(1.0, s)
                bestu = None
                bestc = max(float(np.max(Tp[1:])), float(np.max(Tm[1:])))
                Tpl = float(Tp[1:].max())
                Tml = float(Tm[1:].max())
                bestc = Tpl if Tpl > Tml else Tml
                for u in ugrid:
                    if u < lo - 1e-9 or u > hi + 1e-9:
                        continue
                    du = u - vj
                    if abs(du) < 1e-6 and abs((s - u) - vi) < 1e-6:
                        continue
                    nvap = np.max(Tp[1:] + du * dtp)
                    nvbm = np.max(Tm[1:] + du * dtm)
                    t0 = t0rest + 2.0 * u * (1.0 - u)
                    candmax = max(nvap, nvbm, t0)
                    if candmax < bestc - 1e-11:
                        bestc = candmax
                        bestu = float(u)
                if bestu is not None:
                    du = bestu - vj
                    Tp[1:] += du * dtp
                    Tm[1:] += du * dtm
                    h[i] = s - bestu
                    h[j] = bestu
                    anyim = True
        if not anyim:
            break


def eval_bit(n, A):
    lower = 0
    fm = (1 << n) - 1
    for k in range(1, n):
        F = (A & (A >> k)).bit_count()
        S = (A & (fm ^ ((1 << k) - 1))).bit_count()
        P = (A & ((1 << (n - k)) - 1)).bit_count()
        pt = S - F
        pm = P - F
        if pt > lower:
            lower = pt
        if pm > lower:
            lower = pm
    return lower


def exhaustive_search(n, tcap):
    from itertools import combinations
    k = n // 2
    bestA = 0
    bestT = 10**9
    t0 = time.time()
    idxs = list(range(n))
    for combo in combinations(idxs, k):
        A = 0
        for p in combo:
            A |= (1 << p)
        T = eval_bit(n, A)
        if T < bestT:
            bestT = T
            bestA = A
            if bestT <= 1:
                break
        if len(combo) % 20000 == 0 and time.time() - t0 > tcap:
            break
    h = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    for p in range(n):
        h[p] = 1.0 if (bestA >> p) & 1 else 0.0
    return h, bestT


def cand_record(h, n, bestC5, bestH, bestN):
    h = clip_unit(h)
    if abs(float(h.sum()) - 0.5 * n) > 1e-9:
        return bestC5, bestH, bestN
    c = c5_value(h)
    if c < bestC5 - 1e-12:
        bestC5 = c
        bestH = np.array(h, dtype=float)
        bestN = n
    return bestC5, bestH, bestN


def process_n(n, parent, r, t_n, tstop):
    h, Tp, Tm, cur = None, None, None, float("inf")
    starts = [init_tm(n)]
    for sh in (0, 1):
        starts.append(init_bal(n, sh))
    starts.append(init_gold(n, r))
    starts.append(init_ran(n, r))
    starts.append(init_ran(n, r))
    if parent is not None:
        starts.append(initlerp(n, parent, r))
    iters = 16 if n <= 64 else (12 if n <= 128 else (8 if n <= 256 else 5))
    for h0 in starts:
        if time.time() > t_n or time.time() > tstop:
            break
        hc = h0.copy()
        Tpc, Tmc = buildT(n, hc)
        curc = full_sweep(n, hc, Tpc, Tmc, r, iters, t_n)
        if n <= 300:
            attempt_uphill(n, hc, Tpc, Tmc, r, t_n)
            if time.time() < t_n:
                curc = full_sweep(n, hc, Tpc, Tmc, r, max(2, iters // 2), t_n)
            if time.time() < t_n:
                polish(n, hc, Tpc, Tmc, r, 2, t_n)
                if time.time() < t_n:
                    full_sweep(n, hc, Tpc, Tmc, r, max(2, iters // 3), t_n)
        if curc < cur and verifyT(n, hc, Tpc, Tmc) < 1e-5:
            cur = curc
            h = hc.copy()
            Tp, Tm = Tpc.copy(), Tmc.copy()
    if h is None:
        return h
    return h


def run(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    t0 = time.time()
    try:
        budget = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        budget = 1300.0
    budget = max(30.0, budget)
    tstop = t0 + budget - 4.0
    tdeep = tstop - 90.0
    r = np.random.RandomState(seed)

    bestC5 = float("inf")
    bestH = None
    bestN = 0

    parent = None
    try:
        pv = np.asarray(initial_h_values, dtype=float)
        if pv.ndim == 1 and pv.size >= 8 and np.all(np.isfinite(pv)):
            parent = clip_unit(pv)
    except Exception:
        parent = None

    # ------- Anchor: parent construction (safety floor + decimal polish) -------
    if parent is not None and len(parent) % 2 == 0:
        na = len(parent)
        hb = proj_sum(parent, na)
        c = c5_value(hb)
        bestC5, bestH, bestN = cand_record(hb, na, bestC5, bestH, bestN)
        if na <= 400 and time.time() < tdeep:
            Tp, Tm = buildT(na, hb)
            if time.time() < tdeep - 8:
                polish(na, hb, Tp, Tm, r, 3, min(tdeep - 8, tstop))
            bestC5, bestH, bestN = cand_record(hb, na, bestC5, bestH, bestN)
            hs = snap3(hb)
            Tp, Tm = buildT(na, hs)
            full_sweep(na, hs, Tp, Tm, r, 6, tdeep)
            if time.time() < tdeep - 5:
                polish(na, hs, Tp, Tm, r, 2, tdeep - 5)
            bestC5, bestH, bestN = cand_record(hs, na, bestC5, bestH, bestN)

    # ------- Exact brute force for small n (structural wins) -------
    if time.time() < tdeep:
        for n, cap in ((12, 2.0), (14, 2.0), (16, 4.0), (18, 8.0), (20, 8.0),
                       (22, 14.0), (24, 34.0), (26, 90.0)):
            if time.time() >= tdeep:
                break
            nh, nt = exhaustive_search(n, cap)
            bestC5, bestH, bestN = cand_record(nh, n, bestC5, bestH, bestN)

    # ------- Local search across the grid -------
    n_list = [28, 30, 32, 34, 36, 38, 40, 44, 46, 48, 52, 54, 56, 60, 64,
              72, 80, 88, 96, 112, 128, 144, 160, 176, 192, 224, 256, 320]
    for n in n_list:
        if time.time() >= tdeep:
            break
        per = 8.0 if n <= 64 else (13.0 if n <= 256 else 18.0)
        t_n = min(tdeep, time.time() + per)
        res = process_n(n, parent, r, t_n, tstop)
        if res is not None:
            bestC5, bestH, bestN = cand_record(res, n, bestC5, bestH, bestN)

    # ------- Deep exploitation of best -- and known promising -- n values -------
    seen = set()
    focus = []
    if bestN:
        focus.append(bestN)
    for n in (30, 38, 46, 54, 22, 24, 26, 160, 128, 192, 224, 144, bestN if bestN else 0):
        if n not in seen and n:
            focus.append(n)
            seen.add(n)
    for n in focus[:7]:
        if time.time() >= tstop - 25:
            break
        per = 40.0
        t_n = min(tstop - 25, time.time() + per)
        res = process_n(n, parent, r, t_n, tstop)
        if res is not None:
            bestC5, bestH, bestN = cand_record(res, n, bestC5, bestH, bestN)

    # ------- Final validation -------
    if bestH is None:
        n = 96
        bestH = init_bal(n, 0)
        bestN = n
    bestH = proj_sum(bestH, bestN)
    fin = c5_value(bestH)
    if fin <= bestC5 + 1e-9:
        bestC5 = fin
    try:
        ev = evaluate_erdos_solution(bestH)
        if ev is not None and abs(ev - bestC5) <= 1e-7:
            bestC5 = float(ev)
    except Exception:
        pass
    print("FINAL: n=%d C5=%.6f" % (bestN, bestC5))
    return (bestH, bestC5, bestN)
```
